Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for fitting me in. I, um... I really hope I'm not overreacting, it's probably just stress or lack of sleep, you know? But... well, something a little unsettling has been happening at work, and I thought I should probably be responsible and get it checked out. 

I work as a surveillance officer, and lately, during the quiet stretches in the monitor room... I've been hearing these voices. They come through the monitor speakers and say things like, "You missed it, Ruth," or... "People are going to get hurt because of you." 

The part that finally scared me enough to come in is that... well, I checked, and every single channel is completely muted. And none of the other guards hear anything at all. I know it sounds terrible, I'm so sorry. Is that... is that the kind of thing I should be telling you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

Oh, um... they definitely don't sound like me. I know I keep repeating myself about the whole monitor thing, and I'm so sorry if I'm not giving you new information, but... because they sound like they're coming right out of the speakers, they sound like other people. Like a coworker or someone on the other end of a radio. 

When they say, "You missed it, Ruth," it's in a totally different voice, not my own. I think... I think that's really what scared me enough to actually come in. But hearing someone else's voice telling me people are going to get hurt, especially when I know the channels are completely muted... well, it's just very unsettling. 

But I know it has to just be my own brain doing it, right? Since no one else hears it and the equipment is off. It's just my stress making it sound like someone else. Does that make sense? I really hope that answers what you were asking... I want to make sure I'm giving you the right details.

Oh, um... well, it's not... it's not too bad, really. I mean, it's startling, of course, because of where it's coming from, but I try not to let it get to me too much. 

I guess I just feel... a little anxious? Maybe a bit guilty, because they're saying I missed something important. But I always double-check the feeds, and everything is always fine, so I try to just brush it off and keep doing my job. I really pride myself on being good at my work, so I don't let it stop me from doing what I need to do. 

It's just a little stressful, that's all. Nothing I can't handle, I promise. I just wanted to get it checked out to be safe. Is that... is that a normal way to react? I hope I'm not making a bigger deal out of it than I should be.

I've, um... I've made a couple of small mistakes on my shift paperwork recently. Nothing major! Just silly little errors because I'm a bit distracted. And outside of work... I don't know, I just haven't had the energy to do as much as I normally would. I've had to step back from some activities and seeing friends... just because I can't quite keep up the way I used to.

Oh, goodness, no. No, nothing like that, I promise. I know... I know hearing things probably makes it sound like I'm really far gone, but I definitely don't think anyone is out to get me.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I try really hard to keep a positive attitude, I really do. I don't want to complain or be a burden to anyone. But... um, I guess I have been feeling a bit down lately. 

I'm sure it's just a phase, but... most days, it feels like this heavy, gray mood just sort of settles over me before I even get out of bed. It's so silly, and I'm honestly a little embarrassed to admit this, but sometimes I'll just tear up sitting in my car before my shift starts... over absolutely nothing. Just seeing the casino lights from the parking lot can make me start crying. 

I always pull myself together before I walk into the building, though! I promise I don't let anyone see, and I don't let it affect my professionalism. I just... I hope that doesn't make me sound too unstable. I know I should be handling things better. Is it normal to feel that kind of sadness when you're dealing with stress like this?

I'd hate for anyone to think I was being cold or distant. I try very hard to be a caring person. Does that make sense? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... maybe just a little bit? But I definitely still think those activities are wonderful, I really do. I used to always look forward to my salsa class, and playing softball... and I've always loved building PCs or, you know, having everyone over for board games. I really enjoy being a good host and making sure everyone is having fun. 

It's just... lately, I guess I've been a little bad at keeping up with it all. I end up just kind of ignoring the texts from my friends and sitting at home. It almost feels like... like none of it can really reach me right now, if that makes sense? 

I feel terrible about it, because I'd hate for anyone to think I'm ignoring them on purpose or being a bad friend. I'm really trying to get my motivation back for it. I hope it doesn't make me sound lazy or anything? I just... I think I just need a little break to recharge, but I'm sure I'll be back to my old self soon. Is it okay to just need some time to yourself sometimes?

Um... well, maybe just a little bit of guilt? But I think it's just because I care so much about doing a good job, you know? I take my work in the surveillance room very seriously. I want to make sure everyone is safe.

But hearing someone else's voice telling me people are going to get hurt, especially when I know the channels are completely muted... well, it's just very unsettling. \n\nBut I know it has to just be my own brain doing it, right? Since no one else hears it and the equipment is off. It's just my stress making it sound like someone else. Does that make sense? I really hope that answers what you were asking... I want to make sure I'm giving you the right details.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear these voices?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, it's not... it's not too bad, really. I mean, it's startling, of course, because of where it's coming from, but I try not to let it get to me too much. \n\nI guess I just feel... a little anxious? Maybe a bit guilty, because they're saying I missed something important. But I always double-check the feeds, and everything is always fine, so I try to just brush it off and keep doing my job. I really pride myself on being good at my work, so I don't let it stop me from doing what I need to do. \n\nIt's just a little stressful, that's all. Nothing I can't handle, I promise. I just wanted to get it checked out to be safe. Is that... is that a normal way to react? I hope I'm not making a bigger deal out of it than I should be.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices happen every time you are in the monitor room or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... they definitely come and go. It's not constant, I promise. It's really only during those very quiet stretches... when nothing is happening. \n\nI know I've said this already, and I'm so sorry to keep bringing it back to the same thing, but it's just... it's just when I'm sitting there and it's completely silent. That's when I'll hear it through the speakers. Just a voice saying, \"You missed it, Ruth,\" or... or sometimes something a little harsher, like, \"People are going to get hurt because of you.\" And it's so strange because I know for a fact every channel is muted. \n\nBut it's not all the time! I don't want you to think I'm just sitting there listening to things all shift. Most of the time I'm just doing my job perfectly fine. It's just those occasional quiet moments that scared me enough to come in and talk to you. I really hope that's helpful... I want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture so you don't think it's worse than it is. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have the voices affected your daily life or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I don't want to make it sound like I'm falling apart or anything. I mean, I'm still going to work and doing my best. But... I guess it has been a little harder lately to just keep up with everything. \n\nI've, um... I've made a couple of small mistakes on my shift paperwork recently. Nothing major! Just silly little errors because I'm a bit distracted. And outside of work... I don't know, I just haven't had the energy to do as much as I normally would. Just the bare minimum. I haven't really been cooking much, or having people over, or working on any of my little projects around the house. I just... I just don't have it in me right now. But I'm sure it's just a phase, right? Everyone gets a little burnt out sometimes. I just hope I'm not letting people down too much. Is it normal to feel this drained when you're stressed like this?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that someone or something is out to get you or that there's a conspiracy against you?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, nothing like that, I promise. I know... My coworkers are all really good people, and I know they'd never do anything like that. I really just think it's my own stress getting the better of me, you know? I try very hard to stay rational about it all. \n\nI'm not suspicious of anyone, I swear. I just go in, do my job, and trust the people around me. I hope that's a good sign? It's always just those same comments coming through the muted speakers during the quiet shifts, telling me I missed something or that it's my fault people will get hurt. They just comment on my actions, like you said. \n\nI hope that's a relief? I mean, I know it's still not normal to hear them when no one else does, but... it's better that they aren't telling me to do things, right? Am I explaining it okay for you?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear the voices when you're not in the monitor room?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. It's really only at work. I'm so sorry, I feel like a broken record, just giving you the exact same answer again... but like I was saying, it's just during those quiet stretches in the surveillance room. \n\nThat's exactly what scared me enough to make this appointment, because I'm sitting there, looking at the muted channels, and the voices are coming right through those specific monitor speakers. When I go home, or if I'm anywhere else, it's completely fine. Just normal quiet. So, it's really just tied to that one room at work when no one else is around to hear it. \n\nIs that... is that a good sign? I mean, since it's not happening everywhere, maybe it really is just work stress playing tricks on my ears? I hope I'm giving you what you need to figure this out, I really want to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices sound like they are coming from someone else or do they sound like your own voice?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... they definitely don't sound like me. I know I keep repeating myself about the whole monitor thing, and I'm so sorry if I'm not giving you new information, but... because they sound like they're coming right out of the speakers, they sound like other people. Like a coworker or someone on the other end of a radio. \n\nWhen they say, \"You missed it, Ruth,\" it's in a totally different voice, not my own. I think... I think that's really what scared me enough to actually come in. If it just sounded like my own internal voice, I probably would have just brushed it off as me being hard on myself, you know? But hearing someone else's voice telling me people are going to get hurt, especially when I know the channels are completely muted... well, it's just very unsettling. \n\nBut I know it has to just be my own brain doing it, right? Since no one else hears it and the equipment is off. It's just my stress making it sound like someone else. Does that make sense? I really hope that answers what you were asking... I want to make sure I'm giving you the right details.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear these voices?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, it's not... it's not too bad, really. I mean, it's startling, of course, because of where it's coming from, but I try not to let it get to me too much. \n\nI guess I just feel... a little anxious? Maybe a bit guilty, because they're saying I missed something important. But I always double-check the feeds, and everything is always fine, so I try to just brush it off and keep doing my job. I really pride myself on being good at my work, so I don't let it stop me from doing what I need to do. \n\nIt's just a little stressful, that's all. Nothing I can't handle, I promise. I just wanted to get it checked out to be safe. Is that... is that a normal way to react? I hope I'm not making a bigger deal out of it than I should be.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices happen every time you are in the monitor room or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... they definitely come and go. It's not constant, I promise. It's really only during those very quiet stretches... when nothing is happening. \n\nI know I've said this already, and I'm so sorry to keep bringing it back to the same thing, but it's just... it's just when I'm sitting there and it's completely silent. That's when I'll hear it through the speakers. Just a voice saying, \"You missed it, Ruth,\" or... or sometimes something a little harsher, like, \"People are going to get hurt because of you.\" And it's so strange because I know for a fact every channel is muted. \n\nBut it's not all the time! I don't want you to think I'm just sitting there listening to things all shift. Most of the time I'm just doing my job perfectly fine. It's just those occasional quiet moments that scared me enough to come in and talk to you. I really hope that's helpful... I want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture so you don't think it's worse than it is. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have the voices affected your daily life or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I don't want to make it sound like I'm falling apart or anything. I mean, I'm still going to work and doing my best. But... I guess it has been a little harder lately to just keep up with everything. \n\nI've, um... I've made a couple of small mistakes on my shift paperwork recently. Nothing major! Just silly little errors because I'm a bit distracted. And outside of work... I don't know, I just haven't had the energy to do as much as I normally would. I've had to step back from some activities and seeing friends... just because I can't quite keep up the way I used to. \n\nAt home, I'm mostly just getting through the basics, you know? Just the bare minimum. I haven't really been cooking much, or having people over, or working on any of my little projects around the house. I just... I just don't have it in me right now. But I'm sure it's just a phase, right? Everyone gets a little burnt out sometimes. I just hope I'm not letting people down too much. Is it normal to feel this drained when you're stressed like this?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that someone or something is out to get you or that there's a conspiracy against you?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, nothing like that, I promise. I know... I know hearing things probably makes it sound like I'm really far gone, but I definitely don't think anyone is out to get me. \n\nI don't believe in conspiracies or... or that someone is secretly messing with the monitors at work to trick me. My coworkers are all really good people, and I know they'd never do anything like that. I really just think it's my own stress getting the better of me, you know? I try very hard to stay rational about it all. \n\nI'm not suspicious of anyone, I swear. I just go in, do my job, and trust the people around me. I hope that's a good sign? That I know there isn't some big plot against me? I really want to make sure I'm looking at this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your mood or emotional state since the voices started?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I try really hard to keep a positive attitude, I really do. I don't want to complain or be a burden to anyone. But... um, I guess I have been feeling a bit down lately. \n\nI'm sure it's just a phase, but... most days, it feels like this heavy, gray mood just sort of settles over me before I even get out of bed. It's so silly, and I'm honestly a little embarrassed to admit this, but sometimes I'll just tear up sitting in my car before my shift starts... over absolutely nothing. Just seeing the casino lights from the parking lot can make me start crying. \n\nI always pull myself together before I walk into the building, though! I promise I don't let anyone see, and I don't let it affect my professionalism. I just... I hope that doesn't make me sound too unstable. I know I should be handling things better. Is it normal to feel that kind of sadness when you're dealing with stress like this?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant life changes or particularly stressful events recently, outside of what's been happening at work?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I mean, life is just life, right? It has its normal ups and downs, but nothing major has happened recently. \n\nI really try to keep things pretty stable and drama-free outside of work. I haven't moved, or had any big family issues, or anything like that. Everything at home is... well, it's just quiet. I'm just living my normal life, trying to be a good neighbor and a good employee. \n\nI guess that's why this is all so confusing to me. I feel like I shouldn't be struggling this much when I don't have any real reason to be, you know? I really hope I'm not making a big deal out of nothing. It's okay that I don't have some big traumatic event to point to, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so I'm not wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or feel like your thoughts are disjointed or hard to follow?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't believe so. I really try to be as clear as possible when I speak. My job in surveillance requires me to write pretty detailed incident reports, so I always make sure to keep my thoughts organized. I don't ever feel like they're jumbled up or... or hard to follow. \n\nI mean, sometimes I might ramble a little bit just because I'm nervous, but the thoughts themselves are perfectly normal, I promise. I hope I've been making sense to you today? I really want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly and not being confusing. Am I communicating okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been showing less emotion than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I really try to stay warm and approachable, you know? I always make sure to smile and greet people at work, and I try to be as supportive as I can to my friends and family. \n\nI mean, maybe I've been a little quieter than usual just because I've been feeling a bit tired, but I'm definitely still feeling things and expressing them. I'd hate for anyone to think I was being cold or distant. I try very hard to be a caring person. Does that make sense? I hope I don't seem like I'm shutting down or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your emotions have been less intense than they used to be?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I actually feel like I still care about things just as much as I always have. If anything, maybe I just feel a little... well, a little overwhelmed sometimes, but my feelings are definitely still there. \n\nI really try to stay emotionally invested, you know? I'm still trying my best to stay positive.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of self-harm or suicide since the voices started?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, not exactly.
